## 1. Sources

### 1.1 The task statement

The task asked for a new Mathematica notebook 7+1.nb in `D:\Developer\github\7+1` defining a new realization of the octonions and a new representation of the Clifford algebra of an 8-dimensional vector space that mirror, as far as possible, the split octonions and the pseudo-Clifford algebra of 4+4.nb; real numbers and the real 8×8 and 16×16 matrices of 4+4.nb were to be used if possible, otherwise their complexification; examples were to check all work; notation was to be changed and documented where confusing. It fixed the coordinates

```math
\begin{aligned}
\text{4+4.nb:}\quad &X=(x_0,\dots,x_7),\ x_0=\text{hidden space-like},\ x_1,x_2,x_3=\text{3-space}, \\
&\phantom{X=(x_0,\dots,x_7),\ }x_4=\text{time},\ x_5,x_6,x_7=\text{auxiliary time-like}, \\
\text{7+1.nb:}\quad &X=(x_0,\dots,x_7),\ x_0=\text{time (octonion unit)},\ x_1,x_2,x_3=\text{3-space}, \\
&\phantom{X=(x_0,\dots,x_7),\ }x_4,\dots,x_7=\text{auxiliary space-like},
\end{aligned}
```

and gave two definitions. [1A] the octonions: $`e_0=1`$, $`e_i^2=-1`$, Fano plane, Cayley–Dickson doubling $`\mathbb O=\mathbb H\oplus\mathbb H`$, conjugation $`\bar x=x_0-\sum_ix_ie_i`$ with $`\overline{xy}=\bar y\bar x`$, $`N(x)=x\bar x=\sum_Ax_A^2`$, $`N(xy)=N(x)N(y)`$, $`x^{-1}=\bar x/N(x)`$, alternativity, power-associativity. [1B] the Clifford algebra $`Cl(V,Q)=\mathcal T(V)/\mathcal I_Q(V)`$ with $`v^2=Q(v)1`$, $`vw+wv=2B(v,w)1`$, $`B(v,w)=\frac12(Q(v+w)-Q(v)-Q(w))`$, dimension $`2^8=256`$, a classification table for $`p+q=8`$, and $`Cl_8(\mathbb C)\cong\mathbb C(16)`$. Section 4 lists the statements of [1A] and [1B] that had to be corrected.

### 1.3 Literature

- J. C. Baez, *The Octonions*, Bull. Amer. Math. Soc. 39 (2002) 145–205, arXiv:math/0105155, section 2.2 (the page https://math.ucr.edu/home/baez/octonions/node5.html, which the task statement itself cites, was read on 2026-10-26, when 7+1.nb was first written): the Cayley–Dickson product $`(a,b)(c,d)=(ac-db^{*},\ a^{*}d+cb)`$, where Baez writes $`x^{*}`$ for the conjugate $`\bar x`$ and the conjugate of a pair is $`(a,b)^{*}=(a^{*},-b)`$. 7+1.nb proves that its octonions satisfy this product with $`x=a+\ell b`$, $`\ell=e_4`$.
- H. B. Lawson and M.-L. Michelsohn, *Spin Geometry*, Princeton (1989): classification of real Clifford algebras, $`Cl(p+1,q+1)\cong Cl(p,q)\otimes\mathbb R(2)`$. 7+1.nb does not rely on it: it computes the classification directly (section 2.10 below).
- Standard facts used in proofs: Schur's lemma (the intertwiners of an irreducible module form a division algebra; over $`\mathbb C`$, the scalars); $`Cl_8(\mathbb C)\cong\mathbb C(16)`$ has a unique irreducible module $`\mathbb C^{16}`$; Hurwitz's theorem (a unital composition algebra of dimension 8 is the octonions if its norm is positive definite and the split octonions if the norm is indefinite); Degen's eight-square identity.

### 1.4 The signature: the author’s explanation of 27 October 2026

**Source.** On 27 October 2026 the author of the project explained that the xAct list $`\{7,1,0\}`$ declares seven space-like dimensions and one time-like dimension, while $`\mathrm{diag}(+1,-1,\dots,-1)`$ is simply the squares of the octonion basis, and asked that this be made clear in all documentation, examples and provenance records. **What it corrected.** The version of 7+1.nb pushed in the commits 52d8729 to dc2f178 of github.com/once-ere/7plus1 declared the xAct metric of section 12 with `DefMetric[{1, 7, 0}, …]`, reading the list as the signs of $`\eta^{(1,7)}`$; since then section 12 declares `DefMetric[{7, 1, 0}, g71[-a, -b], CD, …]`, exactly as the author’s notebook Einstein-Lovelock-7+1-Nash.nb does. **What was added.** Checks in 7+1.nb — section 4.7: $`\eta^{(1,7)}`$ is the squares of the octonion basis, $`e_Ae_A=\eta^{(1,7)}_{AA}e_0`$ and $`\mathrm{Re}(e_Ae_B)=\eta^{(1,7)}_{AB}`$; section 12.2: `SignatureOfMetric[g71]` $`=\{7,1,0\}`$ with `SignDetOfMetric[g71]` $`=-1`$, $`\mathrm{Re}(e_Ae_B)=\eta^{(1,7)}_{AB}`$, and $`\mathrm{Re}(x\,x)=\eta^{(1,7)}_{AB}x^Ax^B`$; section 17: the flat metric $`\mathrm{diag}(+1,-1,-1,-1)`$ of the world brane is the squares of the basis $`e_0,e_1,e_2,e_3`$ of the quaternion subalgebra — and the same statements in the Python programs `tools/ref71.py`, `tools/ref_gravity.py` and `tools/ref_sm.py`; the explanation itself in the text of 7+1.nb (sections 1.2, 1.4, 4.2, 4.7, 7, 11, 12.2, 13, 15.1, 17) and in every document, symbol file and provenance file of the repository. **What did not change.** No result. Compared output by output with the backup below, every output of 7+1.nb is the same, except the declared list itself, the new checks and the check counts (and, as in every run, the name of the private context and the measured times); in particular every component of every Lovelock tensor is unchanged. The components follow from the component matrix of the metric, and the sign of the determinant, `SignDetOfMetric`, is $`-1`$ for both lists. **Backup.** The evaluated notebook as it was before is kept in the workspace as `backups/7+1_2026-10-27_before_signature.nb` (the folder backups/ is excluded from the repository by .gitignore), 1034401 bytes, SHA-256 `060942488caf4f45120ffd37be0123e96992a31cd7fdbde09ee4dc4ced4f5d3a`; `tools/verify_7p1_nb.py` compares every input, text and section cell of 7+1.nb with it and finds every cell present, each one unchanged or changed by this explanation, and it compares every output in the same way.

**The explanation, in full.** The 7+1 spacetime has one time-like dimension ($`x_0`$, the direction of the octonion unit $`e_0=1`$) and seven space-like dimensions ($`x_1,x_2,x_3`$ ordinary space, $`x_4,\dots,x_7`$ auxiliary space). Two different objects describe this, and neither may be replaced by the other. (i) The tensor package xAct, used in section 12 of 7+1.nb exactly as in the author’s notebook Einstein-Lovelock-7+1-Nash.nb, declares the metric with `DefMetric[{7, 1, 0}, g71[-a, -b], CD, …]`: the list $`\{7,1,0\}`$ declares seven space-like dimensions, one time-like dimension and no degenerate dimension — the numbers of entries $`+1`$ and $`-1`$ of $`\eta^{(7,1)}=\mathrm{diag}(-1,+1,\dots,+1)`$ (`η71`), the same spacetime written in the sign convention of 4+4.nb (space-like $`=+1`$, time-like $`=-1`$). (ii) The matrix $`\eta^{(1,7)}=-\eta^{(7,1)}=\mathrm{diag}(+1,-1,\dots,-1)`$ (`η17`) is simply the squares of the octonion basis: $`e_0^2=+1`$ for the unit $`e_0=1`$ (time) and $`e_i^2=-1`$ for the seven imaginary units $`e_1,\dots,e_7`$ (space); more generally it is the real part of the product of two basis units, and it gives the quadratic form of the octonions:

```math
\begin{gathered}
\eta^{(1,7)}_{AB}=\mathrm{Re}(e_Ae_B),\qquad e_0^2=+1,\qquad e_i^2=-1\ \ (i=1,\dots,7),\\ \mathrm{Re}(x\,x)=x_0^2-x_1^2-\cdots-x_7^2=\eta^{(1,7)}_{AB}\,x^Ax^B\qquad (x=x^Ae_A\in\mathbb O).
\end{gathered}
```

$`\eta^{(1,7)}`$ gives the signs of the Clifford relation $`\Gamma^{(1,7)}_A\Gamma^{(1,7)}_B+\Gamma^{(1,7)}_B\Gamma^{(1,7)}_A=2\,\eta^{(1,7)}_{AB}\,1_{16}`$ of the real 16×16 Dirac matrices: time squares to $`+1_{16}`$ and each of the seven space directions to $`-1_{16}`$, exactly like the octonion units (in the notation $`Cl(p,q)`$, with $`p`$ generators squaring to $`+1`$ and $`q`$ to $`-1`$, the algebra $`Cl(1,7)\cong\mathbb R(16)`$). It also gives the signs of the metric components: $`g_{00}>0`$ along the time-like direction, negative squares along the space-like directions. Every component computed by 7+1.nb follows from the component matrix of the metric; the declared list fixes for xAct the sign of the determinant of the metric, `SignDetOfMetric[g71]` $`=-1`$, which is the same in both sign conventions, since $`\det \eta^{(1,7)}=\det \eta^{(7,1)}=-1`$. 7+1.nb checks `SignatureOfMetric[g71]` $`=\{7,1,0\}`$, `SignDetOfMetric[g71]` $`=-1`$, $`\mathrm{Re}(e_Ae_B)=\eta^{(1,7)}_{AB}`$ and $`\mathrm{Re}(x\,x)=\eta^{(1,7)}_{AB}x^Ax^B`$.

## 2. Derivations: how each new object was obtained

### 2.1 Octonion matrices $`\tau^{O}`$

Origin: the split matrices of 4+4.nb and the observation (checked) that $`\sigma=\tau_1\tau_2\tau_3=\tau_4\tau_5\tau_6\tau_7`$ commutes with $`\tau_1,\tau_2,\tau_3`$, anticommutes with $`\tau_4,\dots,\tau_7`$ and squares to $`1_8`$. Hence

```math
\begin{aligned}
\tau^{O}_0&=1_8,\quad \tau^{O}_A=\tau_A\ (A\le3),\quad \tau^{O}_k=\tau_k\sigma\ (k\ge4),\qquad (\tau_k\sigma)^2=\tau_k\sigma\tau_k\sigma=-\tau_k^2\sigma^2=-1_8, \\
\tau^{O}_h\tau^{O}_k&=\tau_h\tau_k\sigma=-\tau_k\tau_h\sigma=-\tau_k\sigma\tau_h=-\tau^{O}_k\tau^{O}_h,\qquad \tau^{O}_k\tau^{O}_l=\tau_k\sigma\tau_l\sigma=-\tau_k\tau_l=\tau_l\tau_k=-\tau^{O}_l\tau^{O}_k\quad(k\neq l\ge4).
\end{aligned}
```

This is the real counterpart of the complex substitution $`\tau_k\to i\tau_k`$ that turns split octonions into octonions over $`\mathbb C`$.

### 2.2 Octonion vielbein and multiplication constants

Origin: In[294] and In[325] of 4+4.nb with the split spinor metric $`\sigma`$ replaced by the Euclidean $`g^{O}=1_8`$ and the same unit spinor $`u`$:

```math
\begin{aligned}
(E^{O})_{Ba}&=\big(u^{\mathsf T}g^{O}\bar\tau^{O}_B\big)_a,\qquad L^{O}_A=g^{O}_{AA}E^{O}\tau^{O}_A(E^{O})^{-1},\qquad e_Ae_K=\sum_L(L^{O}_A)_{LK}e_L .
\end{aligned}
```

Why the result is the octonion algebra: $`(E^{O})^{-1}e_0=u`$ makes $`e_0`$ a two-sided unit, and $`\tau^{O}(x)\bar\tau^{O}(x)=N(x)1_8`$ together with the orthogonality of $`E^{O}`$ gives $`N(xy)=N(x)N(y)`$. A unital composition algebra of dimension 8 with a positive definite norm is the octonions (Hurwitz). The notebook checks all the laws directly.

### 2.3 Cayley–Dickson form

Origin: Baez 2002, section 2.2. With $`\ell=e_4`$, $`x=a+\ell b`$, $`a=(x_0,\dots,x_3)`$, $`b=(x_4,\dots,x_7)`$:

```math
(a,b)(c,d)=\big(ac+\gamma\,d\bar b,\ \bar a d+cb\big),\qquad \gamma=-1\ (\text{7+1.nb}),\qquad \gamma=+1\ (\text{4+4.nb}).
```

### 2.4 Euclidean mirror $`T^{O}`$

Origin: In[98] of 4+4.nb with $`\tau\to\tau^{O}`$: $`T^{O}_A=\left(\begin{smallmatrix}0&\bar\tau^{O}_A\\\tau^{O}_A&0\end{smallmatrix}\right)`$, so that $`\lbrace T^{O}_A,T^{O}_B\rbrace=\left(\begin{smallmatrix}\bar\tau^{O}_A\tau^{O}_B+\bar\tau^{O}_B\tau^{O}_A&0\\0&\tau^{O}_A\bar\tau^{O}_B+\tau^{O}_B\bar\tau^{O}_A\end{smallmatrix}\right)=2\delta_{AB}1_{16}`$. In terms of 4+4.nb: $`T^{O}_A=T_A`$ ($`A\le3`$), $`T^{O}_k=T_kS_{44}`$ ($`k\ge4`$), $`S_{44}=T_4T_5T_6T_7=\mathrm{diag}(\sigma,\sigma)`$.

### 2.5 Real 7+1 generators $`\Gamma^{(1,7)}`$

Origin: the standard construction of a Lorentzian Clifford algebra from a Euclidean one, applied to $`T^{O}`$:

```math
\begin{aligned}
\Gamma^{(1,7)}_0&=T^{O}_0,\qquad \Gamma^{(1,7)}_i=T^{O}_0T^{O}_i,\qquad (T^{O}_0T^{O}_i)^2=-(T^{O}_0)^2(T^{O}_i)^2=-1_{16}, \\
\lbrace T^{O}_0,T^{O}_0T^{O}_i\rbrace&=T^{O}_i+T^{O}_0T^{O}_iT^{O}_0=T^{O}_i-T^{O}_i=0,\qquad \lbrace T^{O}_0T^{O}_i,T^{O}_0T^{O}_j\rbrace=-\lbrace T^{O}_i,T^{O}_j\rbrace=-2\delta_{ij}1_{16}.
\end{aligned}
```

Hence $`\lbrace\Gamma^{(1,7)}_A,\Gamma^{(1,7)}_B\rbrace=2\eta^{(1,7)}_{AB}1_{16}`$ — the generators square like the octonion basis, since $`\eta^{(1,7)}_{AB}=\mathrm{Re}(e_Ae_B)`$ ($`e_0^2=+1`$, $`e_i^2=-1`$) — and $`\Gamma^{(1,7)}(x)^2=\mathrm{Re}(xx)1_{16}`$.

### 2.6 Complexified generators $`\Gamma^{(7,1)}`$

Origin: the task's instruction to use the complexification of the 4+4 matrices if real numbers fail. Only the time block is multiplied by $`i`$: $`\tau^{C}_0=\bar\tau^{C}_0=i1_8`$, $`\tau^{C}_k=\tau^{O}_k`$; then $`\Gamma^{(7,1)}_A=\left(\begin{smallmatrix}0&\bar\tau^{C}_A\\\tau^{C}_A&0\end{smallmatrix}\right)`$, $`(\Gamma^{(7,1)}_0)^2=i^2\,1_{16}=-1_{16}`$, and $`\Gamma^{(7,1)}_A=S(i\Gamma^{(1,7)}_A)S^{-1}`$ with $`S=(1_{16}-i\Gamma^{(1,7)}_0)/\sqrt2`$.

### 2.7 The impossibility theorem

Origin: Schur's lemma and the uniqueness of the irreducible module of $`Cl_8(\mathbb C)\cong\mathbb C(16)`$. The intertwiners $`M`$ with $`M\overline{\Gamma^{(7,1)}_A}=\Gamma^{(7,1)}_AM`$ are computed in 7+1.nb with `NullSpace`; they form the line through $`M_0=\Gamma^{(1,7)}_8=\left(\begin{smallmatrix}0&-1_8\\1_8&0\end{smallmatrix}\right)`$, and $`M_0\overline{M_0}=-1_{16}`$. A real realization $`R_A=T\Gamma^{(7,1)}_AT^{-1}`$ would give an intertwiner $`M=T^{-1}\overline T`$ with $`M\overline M=1_{16}`$, impossible because $`M=cM_0`$ gives $`M\overline M=-|c|^21_{16}`$.

### 2.8 Real 32×32 generators

Origin: realification. $`\widehat\Gamma^{(7,1)}_A=\varepsilon\otimes\Gamma^{(1,7)}_A`$ with $`\varepsilon^2=-1_2`$ gives $`\lbrace\widehat\Gamma^{(7,1)}_A,\widehat\Gamma^{(7,1)}_B\rbrace=\varepsilon^2\otimes2\eta^{(1,7)}_{AB}1_{16}=2\eta^{(7,1)}_{AB}1_{32}`$. Commutant: $`Q_i=\varepsilon\otimes1_{16}`$, $`Q_j=\sigma_1\otimes\Gamma^{(1,7)}_8`$, $`Q_k=\sigma_3\otimes\Gamma^{(1,7)}_8`$, found from $`\varepsilon\sigma_1=-\sigma_1\varepsilon`$, $`\varepsilon\sigma_3=-\sigma_3\varepsilon`$ and $`\Gamma^{(1,7)}_8\Gamma^{(1,7)}_A=-\Gamma^{(1,7)}_A\Gamma^{(1,7)}_8`$.

### 2.9 Examples

so(1,7): $`\Sigma_{AB}=\frac14[\Gamma^{(1,7)}_A,\Gamma^{(1,7)}_B]`$; the commutation relations follow from the Clifford relation. Boost and rotation: $`(\Gamma^{(1,7)}_0\Gamma^{(1,7)}_1)^2=1_{16}`$, $`(\Gamma^{(1,7)}_4\Gamma^{(1,7)}_5)^2=-1_{16}`$, so $`e^{\beta\Sigma_{01}}=\cosh\frac\beta2+\sinh\frac\beta2\Gamma^{(1,7)}_0\Gamma^{(1,7)}_1`$ and $`e^{\theta\Sigma_{45}}=\cos\frac\theta2+\sin\frac\theta2\Gamma^{(1,7)}_4\Gamma^{(1,7)}_5`$. Dirac operator: $`(\Gamma^A\partial_A)^2=\frac12\lbrace\Gamma^A,\Gamma^B\rbrace\partial_A\partial_B=\eta_{(1,7)}^{AB}\partial_A\partial_B`$. Real amplitudes: $`p=(3,2,2,0,0,0,0,0)`$, $`p^{\mathsf T}\eta^{(1,7)}p=1`$, $`\not p^{\,2}=1_{16}`$; the real matrix $`\not p`$ has a real 8-dimensional eigenspace for the eigenvalue $`+1`$.

Real spinor fields. If $`J`$ anticommutes with every $`\Gamma^{(1,7)}_A`$, then $`J\Gamma^{(1,7)}_8`$ commutes with every $`\Gamma^{(1,7)}_A`$ (because $`\Gamma^{(1,7)}_8`$ anticommutes with each of them), so by irreducibility $`J\Gamma^{(1,7)}_8=c\,1_{16}`$ and $`J=-c\,\Gamma^{(1,7)}_8`$ (as $`(\Gamma^{(1,7)}_8)^{-1}=-\Gamma^{(1,7)}_8`$), whose square is $`-c^2\,1_{16}`$. Hence the real operator $`\Gamma^A\partial_A-mJ`$ has the square $`\partial_0^2-\dots-\partial_7^2-c^2m^2`$ (a tachyon), and a real equation $`(G^A\partial_A-m)\Psi=0`$ implies the Klein–Gordon equation only if the real $`G_A`$ obey the $`\eta^{(7,1)}`$ relation, impossible for 16×16 matrices (2.7). Massless: for $`k=(1,1,0,\dots,0)`$, $`\not k^{\,2}=k^{\mathsf T}\eta^{(1,7)}k\,1_{16}=0`$ and $`\Gamma^A\partial_A[w\cos(k\cdot x)]=-\sin(k\cdot x)\,\not k\,w`$, which vanishes for the 8 real solutions of $`\not k\,w=0`$. Massive, with the real 32×32 matrices: $`P=\sum_Ap^A\widehat\Gamma^{(7,1)}_A`$, $`P^2=-m^2\,1_{32}`$, and with $`\phi=p\cdot x`$, $`\widehat\Gamma^A=\eta_{(7,1)}^{AA}\widehat\Gamma^{(7,1)}_A`$:

```math
\begin{aligned}
\widehat\Gamma^{A}\partial_A\Big(w\cos\phi+\tfrac1mPw\sin\phi\Big)&=-P\Big(-w\sin\phi+\tfrac1mPw\cos\phi\Big) \\
&=Pw\sin\phi+m\,w\cos\phi=m\Big(w\cos\phi+\tfrac1mPw\sin\phi\Big),
\end{aligned}
```

using $`\partial_A\phi=(\eta^{(1,7)}p)_A`$ and $`\eta_{(7,1)}^{AA}\eta^{(1,7)}_{AA}=-1`$.

### 2.10 Classification of the nine signatures

For each $`p`$, complex generators $`G_A=T^{O}_A`$ ($`A<p`$), $`G_A=iT^{O}_A`$ ($`A\ge p`$) satisfy $`\lbrace G_A,G_B\rbrace=\pm2\delta_{AB}1_{16}`$ with $`p`$ plus signs. The indicator sign of $`M\overline M`$ ($`M\overline{G_A}=G_AM`$) is $`+1`$ for $`p\in\lbrace8,5,4,1,0\rbrace`$ ($`\mathbb R(16)`$) and $`-1`$ for $`p\in\lbrace7,6,3,2\rbrace`$ ($`\mathbb H(8)`$). An independent numerical method (`tools/classify_clifford.py`: commutant of the realified 32-dimensional representation, whose traceless part has a negative definite trace form exactly in the quaternionic case) gives the same table. Explicit real generators: $`T^{O}`$ (8,0), $`\Gamma^{(5,3)}_0=T_0`$, $`\Gamma^{(5,3)}_i=T_0T_i`$ (5,3) with $`\eta^{(5,3)}=\mathrm{diag}(1,-1,-1,-1,1,1,1,1)`$, $`T`$ (4,4), $`\Gamma^{(1,7)}`$ (1,7), $`\Gamma^{(0,8)}_A=T^{O}_8T^{O}_A`$ (0,8).

## 5. Process and environment

- Workspace: `D:\Developer\github\7+1` on Windows; Python and LaTeX ran in a Linux shell with that folder mounted. Intermediate files go to `build/`, which the repository's `.gitignore` excludes.
- The notebook is generated by `tools/make_7p1_nb.py` (cells written with `tools/nbwrite.py`), evaluated in place by `tools/run_eval.wl` in Wolfram 15.0.1, and cross-checked by `tools/verify_7p1_nb.py`.

## 6. The second stage: sections 7–10 of 7+1.nb

This section records the provenance of sections 7–10 of 7+1.nb: the Dirac equation and the Kohn–Sham theories of the field dirac16complex. Notation ($`\Psi`$ is the field dirac16complex):

```math
\begin{gathered}
\Gamma_A=\Gamma^{(1,7)}_A,\qquad \Gamma^{A}=\eta_{(1,7)}^{AA}\Gamma_A,\qquad \Gamma^0=\Gamma_0,\qquad \chi=\chi^{(1,7)}=i\Gamma^{(1,7)}_8,\\ \bar\Psi=\Psi^\dagger\Gamma^0,\qquad \vec x=(x_1,\dots,x_7),\qquad \vec p=(p^1,\dots,p^7).
\end{gathered}
```

### 6.1 The task

1. Back up 7+1.nb and append to it; the first part stays unchanged.
2. A checked Dirac equation on the real 7+1 spacetime for a 16-component quantized fermion field named dirac16complex whose components are complex-valued Grassmann operators, with eight PURELY REAL anticommuting 16×16 Dirac matrices taken from 7+1.nb.
3. Chiral projections writing the 16-component equation as two 8-component equations, coupled when the mass is not zero.
4. A 16-component Kohn–Sham theory of the field coupled to itself, and a chiral version using the chiral projections.
5. Complete, checked documentation (every document with a provenance file and a symbol file, Markdown and LaTeX, compiled to PDF, each self-contained); every completed stage pushed to github.com/once-ere/7plus1 (the repository was renamed from 7+1 to 7plus1); only the Wolfram front end 15.0.1 is used.

### 6.2 The first part of the notebook

The evaluated notebook of the first stage was copied, in the workspace, to the file below (the folder backups/ is excluded from the repository by its .gitignore). `tools/verify_7p1_nb.py` compares every input, text and section cell of sections 0–6 of the new 7+1.nb with that copy: every cell is present, and each is identical except the cells changed or added by the explanation of the signature of 27 October 2026 (section 1.4).

```text
backups/7+1_2026-10-26_before_dirac16complex.nb
306963 bytes
SHA-256 f7abe7279b5540e914de876dc055c199bd491b9c2d9522bf28dbc23689e237ce
```

### 6.3 Derivations and decisions

**Dirac matrices.** The real generators $`\Gamma^{(1,7)}_A`$ of section 2.5 are used unchanged: $`\Gamma_0=\left(\begin{smallmatrix}0&1_8\\1_8&0\end{smallmatrix}\right)`$, $`\Gamma_i=\left(\begin{smallmatrix}\tau^{O}_i&0\\0&-\tau^{O}_i\end{smallmatrix}\right)`$, $`\Gamma_A\Gamma_B+\Gamma_B\Gamma_A=2\eta^{(1,7)}_{AB}1_{16}`$. The opposite sign convention $`\eta^{(7,1)}`$ has no real 16×16 realization (section 2.7), so this is the only real choice. **The factor $`i`$.** With real $`\Gamma_A`$, $`(\Gamma^A\partial_A)^2=\frac12\lbrace\Gamma^A,\Gamma^B\rbrace\partial_A\partial_B=\partial_0^2-\partial_1^2-\dots-\partial_7^2`$, hence

```math
\begin{aligned}
\big(i\Gamma^{A}\partial_A+m\big)\big(i\Gamma^{A}\partial_A-m\big)&=-\big(\partial_0^2-\partial_1^2-\dots-\partial_7^2+m^2\big)1_{16}, \\
\Gamma^0\big(i\Gamma^{A}\partial_A-m\big)&=i\partial_0-H_{\rm D},\qquad H_{\rm D}=-i\Gamma^0\Gamma^i\partial_i+m\Gamma^0 .
\end{aligned}
```

**Real amplitudes.** For $`e^{-i(Ex_0-\vec p\cdot\vec x)}`$, with $`P_\tau=p^i\tau^{O}_i`$ ($`P_\tau^{\mathsf T}=-P_\tau`$, $`P_\tau^2=-\vec p^{\,2}1_8`$):

```math
\begin{aligned}
H_{\rm D}(\vec p)&=\begin{pmatrix}0&m1_8+P_\tau\\m1_8-P_\tau&0\end{pmatrix},\qquad (m1_8+P_\tau)(m1_8-P_\tau)=(m^2+\vec p^{\,2})1_8=E^21_8, \\
H_{\rm D}(\vec p)\begin{pmatrix}E\,e_s\\(m1_8-P_\tau)e_s\end{pmatrix}&=\begin{pmatrix}E^2e_s\\E(m1_8-P_\tau)e_s\end{pmatrix}=E\begin{pmatrix}E\,e_s\\(m1_8-P_\tau)e_s\end{pmatrix}, \\
w_s&=\frac1{\sqrt E}\begin{pmatrix}E\,e_s\\(m1_8-P_\tau)e_s\end{pmatrix},\qquad w_s^{\mathsf T}w_r=2E\,\delta_{sr}.
\end{aligned}
```

**Chirality.** The real matrices anticommuting with every $`\Gamma_A`$ are $`c\,\Gamma^{(1,7)}_8`$ (section 2.9), with square $`-c^21_{16}`$; the chirality is therefore the complex $`\chi=i\Gamma^{(1,7)}_8=\left(\begin{smallmatrix}0&-i1_8\\i1_8&0\end{smallmatrix}\right)`$. With $`E_\pm`$ the 16×8 matrices $`\frac1{\sqrt2}\left(\begin{smallmatrix}1_8\\-i1_8\end{smallmatrix}\right)`$ and $`\frac1{\sqrt2}\left(\begin{smallmatrix}-i1_8\\1_8\end{smallmatrix}\right)`$ ($`\chi E_-=-E_-`$, $`\chi E_+=E_+`$, $`E_-E_-^\dagger=P^{(1,7)}_L`$, $`E_+E_+^\dagger=P^{(1,7)}_R`$), $`\Psi_L=E_-^\dagger\Psi`$, $`\Psi_R=E_+^\dagger\Psi`$ and

```math
\begin{aligned}
E_-^\dagger\Gamma^0E_+&=E_+^\dagger\Gamma^0E_-=1_8,\qquad E_\mp^\dagger\Gamma^0E_\mp=0,\qquad E_\mp^\dagger\Gamma^0\Gamma^iE_\mp=\mp i\tau^{O}_i,\qquad E_-^\dagger\Gamma^0\Gamma^iE_+=0, \\
E_-^\dagger\Gamma^0\big(i\Gamma^{A}\partial_A-m\big)\Psi&=\big(i\partial_0+\tau^{O}_i\partial_i\big)\Psi_L-m\Psi_R,\qquad E_+^\dagger\Gamma^0\big(i\Gamma^{A}\partial_A-m\big)\Psi=\big(i\partial_0-\tau^{O}_i\partial_i\big)\Psi_R-m\Psi_L .
\end{aligned}
```

**Self-coupling.** The four Lorentz-invariant contact interactions without derivatives used in relativistic point-coupling models (scalar, pseudoscalar, vector, axial vector) with real couplings, and real sources that define the densities:

```math
\begin{aligned}
\mathcal L&=\bar\Psi\big(i\Gamma^{A}\partial_A-m\big)\Psi-\bar\Psi\big(\mathsf s+i\chi\mathsf p+\Gamma^{A}(\mathsf v_A+\chi\mathsf a_A)\big)\Psi \\
&\quad-\frac{g_S}2(\bar\Psi\Psi)^2-\frac{g_P}2(\bar\Psi i\chi\Psi)^2-\frac{g_V}2(\bar\Psi\Gamma^{A}\Psi)(\bar\Psi\Gamma_A\Psi)-\frac{g_A}2(\bar\Psi\Gamma^{A}\chi\Psi)(\bar\Psi\Gamma_A\chi\Psi).
\end{aligned}
```

The tensor and higher-rank channels are not included. With $`\hbar=c=1`$ every $`g`$ has mass dimension $`-6`$ (an effective theory: no-sea approximation, or a momentum cutoff for the sea). **Hohenberg–Kohn.** For $`H[w]=H_0+H_{\rm int}+\int w\cdot n`$ with $`w\cdot n=\mathsf s\rho_S+\mathsf p\rho_P+\mathsf v_Aj^A+\mathsf a_Aj_5^A`$, two sources $`w\neq w'`$ with different ground states and the same densities would give $`E<E'+\int(w-w')\cdot n`$ and $`E'<E+\int(w'-w)\cdot n`$, so $`E+E'<E+E'`$; hence $`E[w]=\min_n(F[n]+\int w\cdot n)`$. **Kohn–Sham.** Varying $`T_s+\int w\cdot n+E_H+E_{xc}`$ with respect to $`\Phi_k^\dagger`$ under $`\int\Phi_k^\dagger\Phi_l=\delta_{kl}`$ (multipliers $`\lambda_k`$), with $`\delta\rho_S/\delta\Phi^\dagger=\Gamma^0\Phi`$, $`\delta\rho_P/\delta\Phi^\dagger=\Gamma^0i\chi\Phi`$, $`\delta j^A/\delta\Phi^\dagger=\Gamma^0\Gamma^A\Phi`$, $`\delta j_5^A/\delta\Phi^\dagger=\Gamma^0\Gamma^A\chi\Phi`$, gives $`H_{\rm KS}\Phi_k=\lambda_k\Phi_k`$ with

```math
\begin{aligned}
H_{\rm KS}&=\Gamma^0\Gamma^i(-i\partial_i+\mathcal V_i)+\Gamma^0(m+\mathcal S)+\mathcal V_0+\Gamma^0i\chi\mathcal P+\chi\mathcal A_0+\Gamma^0\Gamma^i\chi\mathcal A_i, \\
\mathcal S&=\mathsf s+g_S\rho_S+\frac{\delta E_{xc}}{\delta\rho_S},\qquad \mathcal P=\mathsf p+g_P\rho_P+\frac{\delta E_{xc}}{\delta\rho_P}, \\
\mathcal V_A&=\mathsf v_A+g_Vj_A+\frac{\delta E_{xc}}{\delta j^A},\qquad \mathcal A_A=\mathsf a_A+g_Aj_{5A}+\frac{\delta E_{xc}}{\delta j_5^A},
\end{aligned}
```

where $`E_H=\int[\frac{g_S}2\rho_S^2+\frac{g_P}2\rho_P^2+\frac{g_V}2j^Aj_A+\frac{g_A}2j_5^Aj_{5A}]`$ and $`T_s=\sum_k\int\Phi_k^\dagger H_{\rm D}\Phi_k`$. **Decision (no sea):** the orbitals are the occupied positive-energy states. **Exchange.** Wick’s theorem for a Slater determinant, $`\langle c_a^\dagger c_c^\dagger c_dc_b\rangle=\varrho_{ba}\varrho_{dc}-\varrho_{da}\varrho_{bc}`$ with $`\varrho_{xy}=\sum_k\Phi_{k,x}\Phi_{k,y}^*`$ and $`\mathcal R=\varrho\,\Gamma^0`$, gives $`\langle:(\bar\Psi O_1\Psi)(\bar\Psi O_2\Psi):\rangle=\mathrm{tr}(O_1\mathcal R)\mathrm{tr}(O_2\mathcal R)-\mathrm{tr}(O_1\mathcal RO_2\mathcal R)`$, hence the exact exchange energy

```math
E_x=-\frac{g_S}2\mathrm{tr}(\mathcal R\mathcal R)-\frac{g_P}2\mathrm{tr}(i\chi\mathcal R\,i\chi\mathcal R)-\frac{g_V}2\mathrm{tr}(\Gamma^{A}\mathcal R\Gamma_A\mathcal R)-\frac{g_A}2\mathrm{tr}(\Gamma^{A}\chi\mathcal R\Gamma_A\chi\mathcal R).
```

**Chiral Kohn–Sham.** With $`\Phi_{kL}=E_-^\dagger\Phi_k`$, $`\Phi_{kR}=E_+^\dagger\Phi_k`$ and the blocks above, $`\kappa=\sum_k\Phi_{kL}^\dagger\Phi_{kR}`$, $`\rho_S=\kappa+\kappa^*`$, $`\rho_P=i(\kappa-\kappa^*)`$, $`\mathcal M=m+\mathcal S+i\mathcal P`$, $`\mathcal V^{L,R}_A=\mathcal V_A\mp\mathcal A_A`$:

```math
\begin{aligned}
\big[\mathcal V^L_0-\tau^{O}_i\partial_i-i\tau^{O}_i\mathcal V^L_i\big]\Phi_L+\mathcal M\Phi_R&=\lambda\Phi_L, \\
\mathcal M^*\Phi_L+\big[\mathcal V^R_0+\tau^{O}_i\partial_i+i\tau^{O}_i\mathcal V^R_i\big]\Phi_R&=\lambda\Phi_R, \\
e^{i\vartheta\chi}&=\cos\vartheta\,1_{16}-\sin\vartheta\,\Gamma^{(1,7)}_8:\qquad \kappa\to e^{2i\vartheta}\kappa, \\
\Delta E_H&=\frac{g_S-g_P}2\big[(e^{4i\vartheta}-1)\kappa^2+(e^{-4i\vartheta}-1)\kappa^{*2}\big].
\end{aligned}
```

**Uniform matter.** Units $`m=1`$; all 8 spin states filled to $`k_F`$; $`|S^6|=16\pi^3/15`$ is the area of the unit sphere in $`\mathbb R^7`$ and $`c_7=8|S^6|/(2\pi)^7=1/(15\pi^4)`$. With the energy density $`\mathcal E`$, its kinetic part $`\mathcal E_{\rm kin}`$, $`\rho_S`$ and the density $`\rho`$:

```math
\begin{aligned}
\rho&=\frac{k_F^7}{105\pi^4},\qquad \rho_S=c_7\int_0^{k_F}\frac{k^6m^*\,dk}{\sqrt{k^2+m^{*2}}},\qquad \mathcal E_{\rm kin}=c_7\int_0^{k_F}k^6\sqrt{k^2+m^{*2}}\,dk, \\
m^*&=m+g_S\rho_S(m^*),\qquad \mathcal E=\mathcal E_{\rm kin}-\frac{(m^*-m)^2}{2g_S}+\frac{g_V}2\rho^2, \\
E/A&=\frac{\mathcal E}{\rho},\qquad E_F=g_V\rho+\sqrt{k_F^2+m^{*2}} .
\end{aligned}
```

Because $`\partial\mathcal E/\partial m^*=0`$ is the gap equation, $`d\mathcal E/d\rho=E_F`$ at every density, and $`\frac{d}{dk_F}\frac{\mathcal E}\rho=\frac{c_7k_F^6}{\rho}(E_F-\frac{\mathcal E}{\rho})`$ vanishes at saturation: $`E/A=E_F`$ (Hugenholtz–Van Hove). **Decisions:** $`g_S=-10^5`$, $`g_V=10^4`$, $`g_P=g_A=0`$ (binding and saturation); root finding by Brent’s method with 50-digit (gap equation) and 40-digit (saturation) working precision. **Dirac sea.** With a cutoff $`\Lambda_{\rm c}`$, $`g_S=g_P=g`$ and a uniform $`\mathcal M`$:

```math
\begin{aligned}
J(M)&=\frac1{120\pi^4}\int_0^{\Lambda_{\rm c}}\frac{q^6dq}{\sqrt{q^2+M^2}},\qquad K(M)=\frac1{120\pi^4}\int_0^{\Lambda_{\rm c}}q^6\sqrt{q^2+M^2}\,dq, \\
\kappa&=-4\mathcal M^*J(|\mathcal M|),\qquad \mathcal M=m-8gJ(|\mathcal M|)\mathcal M,\qquad g_c=-\frac1{8J(0)}=-\frac{90\pi^4}{\Lambda_{\rm c}^6}, \\
\mathcal E_{\rm vac}&=-8K+8J\,\mathrm{Re}[(\mathcal M-m)\mathcal M^*]+32g|\mathcal M|^2J^2 .
\end{aligned}
```

**Decisions:** $`\Lambda_{\rm c}=1`$, $`g=\frac65g_c`$, and $`m=\frac1{100}`$ for the explicit-breaking example.

### 6.4 Literature used in the second stage

- P. A. M. Dirac, Proc. R. Soc. Lond. A 117 (1928) 610 — $`(i\gamma^\mu\partial_\mu-m)\psi=0`$, $`\gamma^\mu\gamma^\nu+\gamma^\nu\gamma^\mu=2\eta^{\mu\nu}`$.
- P. Hohenberg and W. Kohn, Phys. Rev. 136 (1964) B864 — $`E[v]=\min_n(F[n]+\int vn)`$; W. Kohn and L. J. Sham, Phys. Rev. 140 (1965) A1133 — $`[-\frac12\nabla^2+v+v_H+v_{xc}]\varphi_k=\varepsilon_k\varphi_k`$, $`n=\sum_k|\varphi_k|^2`$ (atomic units).
- A. K. Rajagopal and J. Callaway, Phys. Rev. B 7 (1973) 1912; R. M. Dreizler and E. K. U. Gross, *Density Functional Theory*, Springer (1990) — the energy as a functional of the current $`j^A`$, the Dirac form of the Kohn–Sham equations, the no-sea approximation.
- G. C. Wick, Phys. Rev. 80 (1950) 268; M. Fierz, Z. Phys. 104 (1937) 553 — contractions in a Slater determinant (above), and $`\sum_K(\Gamma_K)_{ab}(\Gamma_K^{-1})_{cd}=16\delta_{ad}\delta_{cb}`$ for the 256 products $`\Gamma_K`$ of the generators.
- J. D. Walecka, Ann. Phys. 83 (1974) 491 — mean-field saturation of uniform matter, $`m^*=m-\frac{g_\sigma^2}{m_\sigma^2}\rho_S`$, $`V_0=\frac{g_\omega^2}{m_\omega^2}\rho`$ (here $`g_S=-g_\sigma^2/m_\sigma^2`$, $`g_V=g_\omega^2/m_\omega^2`$); N. M. Hugenholtz and L. Van Hove, Physica 24 (1958) 363 — $`E/A=E_F`$ at saturation.
- Y. Nambu and G. Jona-Lasinio, Phys. Rev. 122 (1961) 345 — $`M=m-2G\langle\bar\psi\psi\rangle`$ with a cutoff and a critical coupling (here $`G=-g/2`$).

### 6.5 Verification of the second stage

Sections 7–9 of 7+1.nb contain 74 checks, all True; they are rows of section 4.2 above, and the evaluation is the one of section 4.1. `tools/ref_dirac16.py` recomputes the constructions independently with 90 boolean checks, all True, and its output is printed in section 4.3, together with that of `tools/verify_7p1_nb.py`, which compares the printed spinors, the closed-form integrals and 23 printed numbers with it (at least 17 digits) and the first part of the notebook with the backup.

## 7. The third stage: sections 11–19 of 7+1.nb

This section records the provenance of the gravity, Kohn–Sham and Standard Model sections (11–19) and of the final summary (then section 20, now section 27). Notation: $`\Gamma_A=\Gamma^{(1,7)}_A`$, $`\Gamma^A=\eta_{(1,7)}^{AA}\Gamma_A`$, $`\eta=\mathrm{diag}(+1,-1,\dots,-1)`$ (simply the squares of the octonion basis, $`\eta_{AB}=\mathrm{Re}(e_Ae_B)`$), $`\Psi`$ the field dirac16complex.

### 7.1 The task

1. Rebuild the ideas of github.com/once-ere/Dirac_claude on the 7+1 spacetime of this project (one time coordinate, seven space coordinates; never the 4+4 metric of Dirac_claude): the Einstein and Lovelock tensors; generalized frame matrices; a canonical spin connection, with a vanishing total covariant derivative, that couples gravity to dirac16complex; the Kohn–Sham theory of dirac16complex in gravitational fields in the primordial (near $`x_0=0`$), modern and far-future epochs; a generalized self-coupling; and the totality of the Standard Model.
2. Compute the Lovelock tensors as in the author’s corrected reference notebook Einstein-Lovelock-7+1-Nash.nb, and do not use its section "FAILED Model metric g71; DO NOT USE!".
3. Document every stage (documentation, provenance and symbol files; Markdown, LaTeX, PDF; self-contained) and push every completed stage to github.com/once-ere/7plus1.
4. Make clear in all documentation, examples and provenance records that the xAct declaration $`\{7,1,0\}`$ declares seven space-like dimensions and one time-like dimension, while $`\mathrm{diag}(+1,-1,\dots,-1)`$ is simply the squares of the octonion basis (the author’s instruction of 27 October 2026).

### 7.2 Inputs and how they were used

The reference notebook Einstein-Lovelock-7+1-Nash.nb (P. L. Nash, 2022, GPL; the author’s corrected version) supplied: the xAct packages xCoba and xTras; the manifold with abstract indices $`a`$, $`b`$, $`c`$, $`d`$, $`f`$, $`i`$, $`j`$, $`k`$, $`l`$, $`m`$, $`n`$, $`p`$, $`q`$, $`r`$, $`s`$, $`y`$; the chart with coordinate scalars `x0[]`, …, `x7[]`; `MyArrayComponents`; the generalized delta `kδ`; the three Lovelock tensors with their normalization

```math
\mathrm{Lovelock}_k=-\frac1{2^{k+1}}\,R_{c_1d_1}{}^{f_1i_1}\cdots R_{c_kd_k}{}^{f_ki_k}\,\delta^{b\,c_1d_1\cdots c_kd_k}_{a\,f_1i_1\cdots f_ki_k}\quad(k=1,2,3);
```

the evaluation of components by `CD = LC[g]`; and the vacuum field equations $`0=-\Lambda\delta+H^{-2}w_1\mathrm{Lovelock}_1+H^{-4}w_2\mathrm{Lovelock}_2+H^{-6}w_3\mathrm{Lovelock}_3`$ with the procedure of solving them for $`w_2,w_3`$. The model metric of its FAILED section was not used anywhere. Taken unchanged as well: the signature declaration `DefMetric[{7, 1, 0}, g71[-a, -b], CD, …]` — in xAct the list $`\{7,1,0\}`$ declares seven space-like dimensions, one time-like dimension and no degenerate dimension, exactly the 7+1 spacetime of this project. It is not to be confused with the matrix $`\eta=\mathrm{diag}(+1,-1,\dots,-1)`$, which is simply the squares of the octonion basis ($`e_0^2=+1`$, $`e_i^2=-1`$, $`\eta_{AB}=\mathrm{Re}(e_Ae_B)`$) and gives the signs of the metric components ($`g_{00}>0`$). **A correction (27 October 2026):** an earlier version of 7+1.nb declared $`\{1,7,0\}`$, reading the list as the signs of $`\eta`$; the author pointed out that $`\{7,1,0\}`$ declares seven space-like and one time-like dimension while $`\mathrm{diag}(+1,-1,\dots,-1)`$ is simply the squares of the octonion basis, and 7+1.nb now declares $`\{7,1,0\}`$ as the reference notebook does. No Lovelock component changed: they follow from the component matrix of the metric, and `SignDetOfMetric` is $`-1`$ for both lists (checked with xTensor 1.2.0). xAct versions as printed by the notebook: xPerm 1.2.3, xTensor 1.2.0, xCoba 0.8.6, xTras 1.4.2.

```text
Einstein-Lovelock-7+1-Nash.pdf  SHA-256 6580f9fcaff9c7f1337df35a7fce6b4b04c022d0e2d68954e8b3954b30f45125
```

Data of the Standard Model part: Particle Data Group 2024/2025 ($`G_F`$, boson masses, quark masses, CKM parameters), NuFit-6.0 (PMNS parameters, normal ordering, without Super-Kamiokande atmospheric data), Planck 2018 (cosmological parameters).

### 7.3 Decisions

| # | decision | reason |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | Christoffel symbols written $`\{{}^{\lambda}_{\mu\nu}\}`$; Landau–Lifshitz curvature | $`\Gamma`$ is reserved for Dirac matrices; with the signs of $`\eta`$ ($`g_{00}>0`$), $`G^\mu{}_\nu-\bar\Lambda\delta^\mu_\nu=\kappa T^\mu{}_\nu`$, $`T^0{}_0>0`$ |
| 2 | the Lovelock tensors are computed twice (xAct; pair-set sums) | exact agreement is an independent check of the reference method |
| 3 | the exponential solutions are obtained from the xAct components of the cosmological metric | a new xAct run with symbolic exponentials does not finish in reasonable time |
| 4 | the canonical (Levi-Civita) spin connection, $`\Omega_\mu=\frac14\omega_{\mu AB}\Gamma^A\Gamma^B`$ | it is the connection with $`\nabla_\mu\Gamma_\nu=0`$ |
| 5 | the self-coupling is organized by the nine ranks of the Clifford algebra | each rank is one Lorentz-invariant class; the Fierz matrix closes on them |
| 6 | Kohn–Sham matter: homogeneous, spin-saturated, no Dirac sea, adiabatic | the cosmological spacetime is homogeneous; the expansion rates are small compared with the orbital energies |
| 7 | the Standard Model on a 3+1 brane of a torus of the auxiliary coordinates | the 7+1 analogue of the fold of Dirac_claude |
| 8 | portals are invented local couplings at the brane | the only contact of dirac16complex with the Standard Model besides gravity |

### 7.4 Literature

- D. Lovelock, J. Math. Phys. 12 (1971) 498; D. Lovelock and H. Rund, *Tensors, Differential Forms, and Variational Principles*, Dover (1989), Remark 2 after Eq. (4.38) — the Lovelock tensors (section 12.1).
- J. M. Martín-García et al., xAct: efficient tensor computer algebra for the Wolfram Language (xPerm, xTensor, xCoba), GPL, www.xact.es; T. Nutma, Comput. Phys. Commun. 185 (2014) 1719 (xTras) — the abstract-index computation of section 12.1.
- L. D. Landau and E. M. Lifshitz, *The Classical Theory of Fields*, Pergamon — curvature conventions; E. Kasner, Am. J. Math. 43 (1921) 217 — power-law solutions; G. W. Gibbons and S. W. Hawking, Phys. Rev. D 15 (1977) 2738 — the de Sitter temperature $`H/2\pi`$.
- S. Navas et al. (Particle Data Group), Phys. Rev. D 110 (2024) 030001 and the 2025 update; I. Esteban et al., JHEP 12 (2024) 216 (NuFit-6.0); Planck Collaboration, Astron. Astrophys. 641 (2020) A6 — the data of section 12.5–12.6.
- N. Arkani-Hamed, S. Dimopoulos, G. Dvali, Phys. Lett. B 429 (1998) 263; L. Wolfenstein, Phys. Rev. D 17 (1978) 2369; S. P. Mikheyev and A. Yu. Smirnov, Sov. J. Nucl. Phys. 42 (1985) 913 — extra dimensions, the MSW effect (section 12.5–12.6).

## 8. The fourth stage: sections 20–23 of 7+1.nb

This section records the provenance of the coupled field equations and their solutions in the three gravity regimes (sections 20–23 of 7+1.nb, appended on 27 October 2026; the summary of all checks became section 24).

### 8.1 The task

1. Compute accurate approximate solutions of the coupled field equations of the model for all three gravity regimes; write each and every interaction in equation form; define all symbols; explain and analyze the solutions (summarized; the original wording is the author’s private working material).
2. Document them in a documentation file with a provenance file and a symbol file, in Markdown and LaTeX, the LaTeX compiled to PDF; no document refers the reader to another file, and every equation used is written in the file that uses it.
3. Then analyze whether dirac16complex can be the dark matter and the dark energy, on the basis of these solutions (a separate stage).
4. Push every completed stage to github.com/once-ere/7plus1 and verify the repository.

**Interpretation.** The "three gravity regimes" are the three epochs of the model: primordial (near $`x_0=0`$; inside it the cubic, Gauss–Bonnet and Einstein terms dominate in turn), modern (a stabilized auxiliary space, four-dimensional gravity on the brane) and far future (de Sitter).

### 8.2 Inputs, data and decisions

Inputs from the earlier sections: the closed forms of the Lovelock tensors of the cosmological metric (section 12.6), the pair-set densities (section 12.5), the Kasner forms (section 14.1). Data: Particle Data Group 2024/2025 (masses), Planck 2018 ($`H_0=67.4`$ km/s/Mpc, $`\Omega_m=0.315`$), $`T_{\rm CMB}=2.7255`$ K, $`N_{\rm eff}=3.044`$, $`M_{\rm Pl}=2.435\times10^{18}`$ GeV. Choices: $`M_8=10`$ TeV, $`\alpha_2=1/M_8^2`$, $`\alpha_3=10^{-6}/M_8^4`$, $`m_r=10`$ GeV, $`f_W=M_8^4`$; in regime I units $`\ell_2=1`$, $`\xi=10^{-6}`$, and a massless collisionless dirac16complex gas normalized to $`\kappa\varepsilon=10^{-6}\times3H_a^2`$ at $`a=b=1`$, $`H_a=1`$; the size of a curvature term is the sum of the absolute values of its monomials (in a Kasner regime the net value cancels). Before the new sections were appended the evaluated notebook was copied to `backups/7+1_2026-10-27_before_coupled.nb` (excluded by .gitignore).

### 8.3 Verification of the fourth stage

Sections 20–23 of 7+1.nb contain 48 checks (48 True); they are rows of section 4.2 above. The independent program `tools/ref_coupled.py` (50 boolean checks, 50 True) repeats every derivation and numerical solution with its own code; the 34 groups of numbers printed by both programs agree to a largest relative difference of 0.00032 (tolerances from $`10^{-14}`$ for exact constants to $`2\times10^{-3}`$ for quantities read off long numerical integrations); its complete output is printed in section 4.3.

## 9. Section 24 of 7+1.nb: dirac16complex as dark matter and dark energy

This section records the provenance of section 24 of 7+1.nb (appended on 27 October 2026; the summary of all checks became section 25, and is now section 27).

### 9.1 The task

1. On the basis of the accurate approximate solutions of the coupled field equations in the three gravity regimes, analyze the validity of associating dark matter (and dark energy) with dirac16complex (summarized; the original wording is the author’s private working material).
2. Document the analysis in a documentation file with a provenance file and a symbol file, in Markdown and LaTeX, the LaTeX compiled to PDF; no document refers the reader to another file, and every equation used is written in the file that uses it.
3. Push every completed stage to github.com/once-ere/7plus1 and verify the repository.

### 9.2 Inputs, data and decisions

Inputs from the earlier sections: the stress tensor from the comoving energy (section 20.2), the radion equation (20.3), the gas-dominated regime without stabilizer (21.3), the gas share after capture (22.1), the radion decay rates into brane fields and into the bulk with Jacobi’s $`r_4(k)`$ (22.2), the late universe (22.4), the Standard Model constants (17.3), the uniform Kohn–Sham matter (8.7). Data: Planck 2018; DESI DR2 (arXiv:2503.14738) and Garcia-Quintero et al. (arXiv:2504.18464); LZ (arXiv:2410.17036); Randall et al. (Bullet Cluster); Iršič et al. (Lyman-$`\alpha`$); Ballesteros, Garcia and Pierre (decay-produced dark matter); Alvey et al. (Pauli bound, $`g=2`$, scaling $`g^{-1/4}`$); Roach et al. (NuSTAR); Particle Data Group 2024 (large extra dimensions). Decisions: natural couplings at $`M_8`$; Majorana normalization of the sterile-neutrino decay rates; instantaneous reheating at $`T_{\rm RH}\le T_*`$; production by Higgs decays as a lower bound; continuum Kaluza–Klein sums checked against lattice sums; Pauli bound for $`g=16`$. Before section 24 was appended the evaluated notebook was copied to `backups/7+1_2026-10-27_before_dark.nb` (excluded by .gitignore). One correction to section 23.3 was made: the cold-dark-matter density in its table read "0.265" and now reads "Omega_c = 0.264" ($`0.120/0.674^2=0.26416`$).

### 9.3 Verification

Section 24 of 7+1.nb contains 20 checks (20 True); they are rows of section 4.2 above. The independent program `tools/ref_dark.py` (20 boolean checks, 20 True) recomputes every number with its own code; the 29 groups of numbers printed by both programs agree to a largest relative difference of $`1.4\times10^{-7}`$; its complete output is printed in section 4.3. `tools/verify_7p1_nb.py` confirms that, with section 24 removed, the notebook equals the backup except the documented correction.

## 10. Sections 25–26 of 7+1.nb: the auxiliary torus, the world brane and the radion

This section records the provenance of sections 25 and 26 of 7+1.nb (appended on 28 October 2026; the summary of all checks became section 26 and then section 27).

### 10.1 The task

1. Define and explain the "auxiliary torus" and the "world brane" precisely and correctly, each in its own documentation, symbol and provenance files (Markdown, LaTeX, PDF), for students, with examples (summarized; the original wording is the author’s private working material).
2. Read a note of the author on the radion and use its ideas to verify and clarify the radion and brane documents; create examples in which the four extra space dimensions are a 4-sphere and a 4-torus.
3. Push every completed stage to github.com/once-ere/7plus1 and verify the repository.

### 10.2 Inputs and decisions

Inputs: the cosmological and inhomogeneous metrics and the curvature routine (section 11), the Standard Model and the brane representation (17), the Kaluza–Klein operator (18), the stress tensor, the radion and the stabilizer (20), the regimes (21–23), the portals (24). The note was read from the author’s PDF (not part of the repository). Decisions: the brane at $`\vec y=0`$, not dynamical; the sphere compared at the same volume $`V_\perp`$; the warped comparison in five dimensions; all printed numbers computed, never typed; the Higgs–radion numbers in 60-digit arithmetic. Before each section was appended the evaluated notebook was copied to `backups/7+1_2026-10-28_before_torus_brane.nb` and `backups/7+1_2026-10-28_before_radion.nb` (excluded by .gitignore). The record of the evaluation, which `tools/run_eval.wl` writes to build/eval_log.txt (excluded by .gitignore), is now also kept, with the SHA-256 of the evaluated notebook, in `tools/eval_log_7p1.txt`, so that the documents rebuilt from a fresh clone are identical to the committed ones.

### 10.3 Verification

Sections 25 and 26 contain 27 and 33 checks (27 and 33 True); they are rows of section 4.2 above. The independent programs `tools/ref_torus_brane.py` (38 boolean checks, 38 True) and `tools/ref_radion.py` (31, 31 True) recompute every number with their own code; the 17 and 15 groups of numbers printed by the notebook and by them agree to a largest relative difference of $`3.8\times10^{-10}`$ and $`8.2\times10^{-11}`$; their complete outputs are printed in section 4.3. `tools/verify_7p1_nb.py` confirms that, with each new section removed, the notebook equals its backup.

## 11. The spreadsheet of 28 October 2026

This section records the provenance of the spreadsheet `7plus1_status_2026-10-28.xlsx` and of its three documents, which were added to the repository after commit 102f399.

### 11.1 The task

1. Create a spreadsheet from the report on the clean-up of the loose lines of 28 October 2026 (summarized; the original wording is the author’s private working material).
2. Add the spreadsheet to the repository and push it.

### 11.2 Sources and decisions

The spreadsheet records the state of the repository at commit 102f399: 4 commits, 30 documents with 478 pages, 16 rewritten paragraphs, the results of 12 check programs, the checks of the notebook, a fresh clone rebuilt from scratch, and 4 private files. Its numbers come from `git log` and `git show` (the commits and the changed documents), `pdfinfo` (the pages), the logs of LaTeX of three builds (the loose lines), the outputs of the check programs, `tools/eval_log_7p1.txt` and the evaluated notebook. Decisions: the numbers are kept in a data file and the spreadsheet is written from it by a program; every total and every summary value is a formula, and LibreOffice stores the computed values; the private files are described without their names; the version in the repository is written for its readers and differs from the first version delivered to the author in wording and layout only.

**A correction found while the data were gathered.** The function `parse_ref` of `tools/doc_stage3_common.py` split a line `name : result` at the first “ : ”, so two checks whose names contain “ : ” were not counted; since commit 102f399 it splits such a line at the last “ : ”, and the documents give 50 checks of `tools/ref_coupled.py` and 38 of `tools/ref_torus_brane.py` (before, 49 and 37).

**A correction made with the spreadsheet.** GRAVITY_PROVENANCE said that the author’s reference notebook Einstein-Lovelock-7+1-Nash.nb is not part of the repository; at the author’s request it was added, with its PDF printout, on 27 October 2026 (commit 730a263). The record now says so.

### 11.3 Verification

| check | result |
|---|---|
| the stored values of the formulas of the spreadsheet, against an evaluator of their own | 35 of 35 equal |
| the names and statistics of the commits (`git show --shortstat`) | 4 of 4 agree |
| the page counts of the PDFs at 102f399 (`pdfinfo`) | 30 of 30 agree |
| the formulas of the rewritten paragraphs, in their documents at 102f399 | 14 of 14 found |
| the exit codes and the counts of results of the twelve programs, against this build | 12 of 12 agree |

The counts of results of a program $`P`$ are $`N_{\rm True}(P)`$ and $`N_{\rm False}(P)`$, the numbers of lines of its output that end in “: True” or “(True)”, respectively “: False” or “(False)”. A loose line is a line whose badness $`b=\min\big(10000,100(t/s)^3\big)`$ exceeds 1000, where $`t`$ is the stretch that the line needs and $`s`$ the total stretchability of its spaces.
